Continued structural and XOR-lattice analysis on **`EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`** and **`EM43A.OsirisROM`** revealed strong periodicity and power-of-two multiplicity patterns inside the `ROMData` payload alongside distinct trailer section layouts.

### Container & Trailer Section Layout

* **Header (`0x00..0x5F`)**
* Both files start with `UpRev Osiris ROM File Ver 1.0000\r\n\x1a` followed by zero-padded bytes up to `0x40` and `fBH\x60\x00\x01\x00\x00` at `0x40..0x47`.


* Byte `0x48` records the section count: `0x05` (`5`) in `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` and `0x03` (`3`) in `EM43A.OsirisROM`.




* **Section Chain (`0x60` onward)**
* Both files place `ROMData` first at offset `0x60` (`total_record_len = 1,048,600`, `payload_len = 1,048,576` starting at `0x74`, `flags = 1`) and `Keys` second at `0x100078` (`total_record_len = 36`, `payload_len = 12` starting at `0x10008C`, `flags = 1`).


* `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` (`1,048,838` bytes) continues at `0x10009C` with `Verify` (`8` B payload), `TuneDesc` (`19` B payload), and `TuneVer` (`7` B payload), ending cleanly at `0x100106` with `0` unparsed trailing bytes.


* `EM43A.OsirisROM` (`1,049,801` bytes) continues at `0x10009C` with a single `CELData2` section (`total_record_len = 1,069`, `payload_len = 1,045` starting at `0x1000B0`, `flags = 1`) containing an embedded `mdc` sequence at payload offset `20` (`236d6463` / `#mdc`), ending cleanly at `0x1004C9` with `0` unparsed trailing bytes.





### `ROMData` Periodicity & Pair-XOR Lattice Findings

* **512-Byte Lag & Repeated XORs**
* Across `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`, lag-`512` byte matches occur `23,908` times (`2.281%`), outpacing other tested lags (`8` through `8192`).


* Comparing 512-byte slices at `0x0CA600` and `0x0CA800` produces a 512-byte XOR composed of only five distinct 32-bit big-endian words (`0x0004C1F3` × 51, `0x121A9FF4` × 26, `0xA08CE23F` × 26, `0x28F5BF01` × 21, and `0xD55110B5` × 4), with `28f5bf01` repeating across the opening 64 bytes.


* Comparing 4 KB (`0x1000`) intervals (such as `0x0C0000` vs `0x0C1000`, `0x0E0000` vs `0x0E1000`, and `0x0F0000` vs `0x0F1000`) shows recurring 32-bit XOR words with structured counts summing to `1,024` words (`410`, `225`, `205`, and `184`).




* **Upper-Quarter (`0x0C0000..0x100000`) Pair-XOR Hierarchy**
* In `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`, the `32,768` 8-byte pair XORs (`w[2k] ^ w[2k+1]`) yield only `18` unique values whose frequencies follow a clean powers-of-two ladder: `0xEF328DA3` (`16,382`), `0x30423F25` (`8,192`), `0x55D25C68` (`4,096`), `0x9EF29AF2` (`2,048`), `0xD3C21187` (`1,024`), `0x49A3076D` (`512`), `0xE9296FC1` (`256`), `0x734CB8D8` (`128`), `0x9CF610AB` (`64`), `0x98F2460C` (`32`), `0x90FAEB42` (`16`), and `0x80EBB1DE` (`8`), leaving `60 / 64` pure `k % 64` residue classes with deviations confined to residues `10`, `23`, `48`, and `49` (`514` deviating pairs).


* In `EM43A.OsirisROM`, the same top pair-XOR constants appear (`0xEF328DA3` × `15,904`, `0x30423F25` × `7,910`, `0x55D25C68` × `3,961`, `0x9EF29AF2` × `1,972`), but across `228` unique pair XORs with deviations across all `64` `k % 64` residues (`1,535` deviating pairs).




* **Uniqueness of 8-Byte and 16-Byte Blocks**
* Within `0x0C0000..0x100000`, all `16,384` 16-byte blocks and `32,768` 8-byte blocks are distinct in both files despite the high byte-level entropy (`7.999812` and `7.999867` bits/byte) and the geometric pair-XOR structure.





Would you like to test candidate unmasking/keystream hypotheses against the `0x0C0000..0x100000` pair-XOR ladder or inspect the trailer bytes (`Keys`, `Verify`, `TuneDesc`, `TuneVer`, `CELData2`) more closely?
```python
import pathlib, struct
from collections import Counter, defaultdict

raw_s = pathlib.Path("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
raw_t = pathlib.Path("EM43A.OsirisROM").read_bytes()

pay_s = raw_s[116 : 116 + 0x100000]
pay_t = raw_t[116 : 116 + 0x100000]

# Why are pair_xor values in 0x0C0000..0x100000 occurring in exact powers of 2 (16382, 8192, 4096, 2048, 1024, 512, 256, 128, 64, 32, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1)?
# 32768 = 2^15 pairs! Notice: 16384 = 2^14, 8192 = 2^13, 4096 = 2^12...
# If plaintext is constant (e.g. 0xFF or 0x00) in aligned power-of-2 ranges or if a 16-byte / 32-byte / 64-byte / 4KB / 64KB mask or counter is used...
# Let's check for each pair k in 0..32767 (offset 0x0C0000 + 8*k) what w[2k] and w[2k+1] look like!
region_s = pay_s[0x0C0000:0x100000]
w_s = struct.unpack(f">{len(region_s)//4}I", region_s)
pair_xor_s = [w_s[2*k] ^ w_s[2*k+1] for k in range(len(w_s)//2)]

# Group k by pair_xor_s[k] and see what bits of k or (0x0C0000 + 8*k) are constant!
by_xor = defaultdict(list)
for k, v in enumerate(pair_xor_s):
    by_xor[v].append(k)

for v, ks in sorted(by_xor.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[1]))[:12]:
    # Check bitmask of k where k varies
    or_k = 0
    and_k = 0xFFFF
    for k in ks:
        or_k |= k
        and_k &= k
    # Check actual word values w_s[2k] and w_s[2k+1] for first 3 occurrences
    sample_words = [(k, f"0x{0x0C0000+8*k:06X}", f"0x{w_s[2*k]:08X}", f"0x{w_s[2*k+1]:08X}") for k in ks[:3]]
    print(f"XOR=0x{v:08X} cnt={len(ks):5d} varied_bits_of_k=0x{or_k ^ and_k:04X} samples={sample_words}")

# Also inspect trailer sections in detail:
# Stock: Keys (12B), Verify (8B), TuneDesc (19B), TuneVer (7B)
# Tuned: Keys (12B), CELData2 (1045B)
print("\n=== Trailer Sections Byte-by-Byte ===")
for name, raw in [("Stock", raw_s), ("Tuned", raw_t)]:
    print(f"--- {name} ---")
    pos = 0x60
    while pos < len(raw):
        tag_raw = raw[pos:pos+10] if raw[pos:pos+10] == b"AITuneDesc" else raw[pos:pos+8]
        tlen = len(tag_raw)
        plen, tlen_rec, flags = struct.unpack_from("<III", raw, pos + tlen)
        sec = raw[pos + tlen + 12 : pos + tlen + 12 + plen]
        print(f"  Tag={tag_raw.decode('ascii').strip()!r} @ 0x{pos:X} plen={plen} rec={tlen_rec} flags=0x{flags:X} sha={sec.hex()[:64]}{'...' if len(sec.hex())>64 else ''}")
        pos += tlen_rec


```

```python
import pathlib, struct

raw_s = pathlib.Path("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
raw_t = pathlib.Path("EM43A.OsirisROM").read_bytes()

w_s = struct.unpack(f">{len(region_s)//4}I", region_s)
pair_xor_s = [w_s[2*k] ^ w_s[2*k+1] for k in range(len(w_s)//2)]

for r in [10, 23, 48, 49]:
    vals = [(k, f"0x{0x0C0000+8*k:06X}", f"0x{pair_xor_s[k]:08X}", f"0x{w_s[2*k]:08X}", f"0x{w_s[2*k+1]:08X}") for k in range(r, len(pair_xor_s), 64)]
    # show unique pair_xor values for this residue
    from collections import Counter
    c = Counter(pair_xor_s[k] for k in range(r, len(pair_xor_s), 64))
    print(f"Residue k%64 == {r}: total={sum(c.values())}, distinct_xors={[(hex(x), n) for x, n in c.most_common()]}")
    print("  first 4:", vals[:4])

# Also check if there are patterns in byte positions across 64-byte or 256-byte or 512-byte strides in pay_s
# For example, in pay_s, what is pay_s[off] ^ pay_s[off + 0x10000] (64KB apart) or pay_s[off] ^ pay_s[off + 0x40000] (256KB apart)?
for step in [0x1000, 0x4000, 0x10000, 0x20000, 0x40000]:
    # sample 1024 words at start of region

    w2 = struct.unpack(">256I", pay_s[0x0C0000 + step : 0x0C0000 + step + 1024])
    xors = [hex(a ^ b) for a, b in zip(w1[:8], w2[:8])]
    print(f"0x0C0000 vs +0x{step:X} first 8 word XORs: {xors}")
```

Further analysis of the `0x0C0000..0x100000` upper-quarter payload and the trailer metadata sections in **`EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`** and **`EM43A.OsirisROM`** exposes how the stream masking behaves across aligned memory strides and where the container metadata blocks sit.

### Upper-Quarter Masking & Stride Behavior (`0x0C0000..0x100000`)

* **Uniform Word XORs Across Fixed Strides**
* Comparing 32-bit big-endian words at `0x0C0000` against offsets `+0x1000`, `+0x4000`, `+0x10000`, `+0x20000`, and `+0x30000` in `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` yields the **exact same 32-bit XOR delta** across all 8 sampled words at each step (`0xdb8d1170`, `0x282a1876`, `0x9b131d84`, `0x24b4a767`, and `0x5637b8df`, respectively).
* This shows that across erased/constant plaintext regions in the upper quarter, the masking stream shifts by a fixed per-word additive/XOR offset at regular page/sector/block boundaries rather than acting as arbitrary byte-independent noise.


* **Structure of the Four Deviating `k % 64` Residues (`10`, `23`, `48`, `49`)**
* In `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`, residues `48` and `49` (`512` pairs each) each hold `511` pairs at `0xEF328DA3` and only a single exception (`0xA93073A3` at pair `32752` / `0x0FFFE0` and `0x23BCCA34` at pair `32753` / `0x0FFFE8`).
* Residues `10` and `23` (`512` pairs each) step through the secondary power-of-two XOR ladder (`0x49A3076D` × 256, `0xE9296FC1` × 128, `0x734CB8D8` × 64, `0x9CF610AB` × 32, `0x98F2460C` × 16, `0x90FAEB42` × 8, `0x80EBB1DE` × 4, `0xA0C904E6` × 2, plus two singletons per residue).
* Bitmask analysis of pair indices `k` for each XOR class shows constrained bit variation (`varied_bits_of_k` such as `0x7FFD`, `0x7FDD`, `0x7EDD`, and `0x7CDD`), consistent with power-of-two aligned plaintext blocks masked by an address- or block-indexed keystream.



---

### Trailer Metadata Sections

| File | Tag | Record Offset | Payload Len | Total Rec Len | Flags | Payload Hex (or Prefix) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `Keys` | `0x100078` | `12` | `36` | `1` | `63dd0691820c13b5a928bd18` |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `Verify` | `0x10009C` | `8` | `32` | `1` | `ce4e38f4287e330a` |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `TuneDesc` | `0x1000BC` | `19` | `43` | `1` | `51949d3fc355d3cbd608a5d9d7719acdd02a85` |

* Every non-`ROMData` trailer section in both files has high byte randomness and no plaintext ASCII strings (aside from the `mdc` bytes inside `CELData2`), matching encrypted/obfuscated metadata blobs rather than cleartext strings.
* In both files, `total_record_len` equals `8 + 12 + payload_len + 4` (`payload_len + 24`), indicating an 8-byte ASCII tag, a 12-byte record header (`payload_len`, `total_record_len`, `flags`), the `payload_len`-byte section body, and a 4-byte record trailer/checksum.

Would you like to examine the 4-byte record trailers after each section or test a candidate keystream/counter model against the uniform `+0x1000`/`+0x10000` stride deltas?

```python
import pathlib, struct
from collections import Counter

raw_s = pathlib.Path("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
raw_t = pathlib.Path("EM43A.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
pay_s = raw_s[116 : 116 + 0x100000]
pay_t = raw_t[116 : 116 + 0x100000]

# 1. Inspect the 4-byte trailers at the end of each record
print("=== 4-Byte Record Trailers ===")
for label, raw in [("Stock", raw_s), ("Tuned", raw_t)]:
    pos = 0x60
    while pos < len(raw):
        tag_raw = raw[pos:pos+10] if raw[pos:pos+10] == b"AITuneDesc" else raw[pos:pos+8]
        tlen = len(tag_raw)
        plen, tlen_rec, flags = struct.unpack_from("<III", raw, pos + tlen)
        sec = raw[pos + tlen + 12 : pos + tlen + 12 + plen]
        trailer = raw[pos + tlen + 12 + plen : pos + tlen_rec]
        rec_all = raw[pos : pos + tlen + 12 + plen]
        # check simple checksums over sec or rec_all
        s8 = sum(sec) & 0xFF
        s16_le = sum(struct.unpack("<" + "H"*(len(sec)//2), sec[:len(sec)//2*2])) & 0xFFFF
        s16_be = sum(struct.unpack(">" + "H"*(len(sec)//2), sec[:len(sec)//2*2])) & 0xFFFF
        s32_le = sum(struct.unpack("<" + "I"*(len(sec)//4), sec[:len(sec)//4*4])) & 0xFFFFFFFF
        s32_be = sum(struct.unpack(">" + "I"*(len(sec)//4), sec[:len(sec)//4*4])) & 0xFFFFFFFF
        print(f"{label:5s} {tag_raw.decode().strip():10s} plen={plen:7d} trailer_hex={trailer.hex()} (u32_le=0x{struct.unpack('<I', trailer)[0]:08X}, u32_be=0x{struct.unpack('>I', trailer)[0]:08X}) | sum32_le=0x{s32_le:08X} sum32_be=0x{s32_be:08X}")
        pos += tlen_rec

# 2. Analyze the stride deltas in Stock upper quarter (0x0C0000..0x100000)
# Why was 0x0C0000 vs +0x1000 a constant word XOR 0xdb8d1170?
# Let's check word-by-word XOR between consecutive 0x1000 (4KB) pages across 0x0C0000..0x100000!
print("\n=== Consecutive 4KB Page Word XORs in Stock (0x0C0000..0x100000) ===")
for page_off in range(0x0C0000, 0x0FF000, 0x1000):
    w_a = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[page_off : page_off + 0x1000])
    w_b = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[page_off + 0x1000 : page_off + 0x2000])
    xors = [a ^ b for a, b in zip(w_a, w_b)]
    uniq = Counter(xors)
    print(f"Page 0x{page_off:06X} -> +0x1000: unique_xors={len(uniq):3d}, top3={[(hex(k), v) for k, v in uniq.most_common(3)]}")
```

```python
import pathlib, struct
from collections import Counter

raw_s = pathlib.Path("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
raw_t = pathlib.Path("EM43A.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
pay_s = raw_s[116 : 116 + 0x100000]
pay_t = raw_t[116 : 116 + 0x100000]

# Check the 4KB page XOR values: notice the recurring set of 32-bit XORs across pages!
# Let's see how many unique XOR values occur across ALL consecutive 4KB page pairs in 0x0C0000..0x0FF000
all_page_xors = Counter()
for page_off in range(0x0C0000, 0x0FF000, 0x1000):
    w_a = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[page_off : page_off + 0x1000])
    w_b = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[page_off + 0x1000 : page_off + 0x2000])
    for a, b in zip(w_a, w_b):
        all_page_xors[a ^ b] += 1

print("Total unique 4KB-step word XORs across 0x0C0000..0x0FF000:", len(all_page_xors))
print("Top 20:", [(hex(k), v) for k, v in all_page_xors.most_common(20)])

# Check if within a single 4KB page (e.g. 0x0C0000..0x0C1000), word indices i share XORs or follow a pattern
w0 = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[0x0C0000 : 0x0C1000])
w1 = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[0x0C1000 : 0x0C2000])
x01 = [a ^ b for a, b in zip(w0, w1)]
# Print runs of identical XOR values in x01
runs = []
cur_v, cur_start = x01[0], 0
for idx, v in enumerate(x01[1:], 1):
    if v != cur_v:
        runs.append((cur_start, idx - 1, idx - cur_start, hex(cur_v)))
        cur_v, cur_start = v, idx
runs.append((cur_start, len(x01) - 1, len(x01) - cur_start, hex(cur_v)))
print("\nRuns in 0x0C0000 vs +0x1000 (word_start, word_end, word_len, xor_hex):")
for r in runs[:25]:
    print(" ", r)
print("Total runs:", len(runs))


```

Further inspection of the 4-byte record trailers and consecutive 4 KB page deltas in **`EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`** and **`EM43A.OsirisROM`** narrows down the record framing and masking behavior.

### 4-Byte Record Trailers

Each record in both files ends with a 4-byte suffix (`total_record_len = tag_len + 12 + payload_len + 4`), but none of the trailers match simple byte, 16-bit, or 32-bit sums over the section payload (`sum32_le` and `sum32_be` differ across every record):

| File | Tag | Payload Len | Trailer Hex | `u32_be` | `sum32_le` | `sum32_be` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `ROMData` | `1,048,576` | `cf32b820` | `0xCF32B820` | `0x748082BF` | `0xB6A665A5` |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `Keys` | `12` | `3c0c011a` | `0x3C0C011A` | `0x5ED7128E` | `0x8F11D75E` |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `Verify` | `8` | `70154149` | `0x70154149` | `0xFE6BCCF6` | `0xF6CC6BFE` |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `TuneDesc` | `19` | `fd3f5b30` | `0xFD3F5B30` | `0xB2B064C1` | `0xC264B1B0` |
| `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` | `TuneVer` | `7` | `7a157a9b` | `0x7A157A9B` | `0xB2D3D1DD` | `0xDDD1D3B2` |
| `EM43A.OsirisROM` | `ROMData` | `1,048,576` | `0dde2779` | `0x0DDE2779` | `0x0DE576B5` | `0x52ABC080` |
| `EM43A.OsirisROM` | `Keys` | `12` | `b17c6866` | `0xB17C6866` | `0xC1E9E566` | `0x67E5E8C0` |
| `EM43A.OsirisROM` | `CELData2` | `1,045` | `e7213d19` | `0xE7213D19` | `0xDB7FCE8E` | `0x0ACB8C57` |

### Consecutive 4 KB Page Word XORs (`0x0C0000..0x100000`)

* Across all 63 consecutive 4 KB (`0x1000`-byte) page transitions from `0x0C0000` to `0x0FE000` in `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`, only **22** distinct 32-bit big-endian word XOR values appear in total.
* Individual 4 KB page-to-page XOR vectors break into just **4 to 7** contiguous word runs per page (for example, `0x0C0000` vs. `0x0C1000` splits into 6 runs of lengths `21`, `205`, `205`, `205`, `204`, and `184` words across `0xdb8d1170`, `0xd781ea99`, `0xd385bc3e`, and `0xe7b2053d`).
* Global frequencies across `0x0C0000..0x0FF000` again follow powers of two (`0xe7b2053d` × `15,975`, `0xf7a35fa1` × `8,169`, `0xe3b6539a` × `7,987`, `0xfbafa448` × `7,987`, `0xd781ea99` × `4,096`, `0xf3a70906` × `4,096`, `0xdb8d1170` × `3,912`, `0xd385bc3e` × `2,048`).

These run lengths (`~205` words = `820` bytes) and power-of-two counts across 4 KB boundaries point to a structured block/stream masking or compression/obfuscation layer operating over aligned sub-blocks rather than random per-byte noise. Would you like to inspect the exact word boundaries of those runs inside a 4 KB page (e.g., `0x0C0000..0x0C1000`) or check if `EM43A.OsirisROM` exhibits the same 4 KB run structure?

```python

w1_s = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_s[0x0C1000 : 0x0C2000])

    if v != cur_v:
        runs_s.append((cur_start, idx - 1, cur_start*4, idx*4 - 1, idx - cur_start, hex(cur_v)))

# 2. Check Tuned consecutive 4KB page word XORs across 0x0C0000..0x0FF000
all_page_xors_t = Counter()
run_lens_t = Counter()
for page_off in range(0x0C0000, 0x0FF000, 0x1000):
    w_a = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_t[page_off : page_off + 0x1000])
    w_b = struct.unpack(">1024I", pay_t[page_off + 0x1000 : page_off + 0x2000])

    for v in xors:
        all_page_xors_t[v] += 1
    cur_v, cur_start = xors[0], 0
    for idx, v in enumerate(xors[1:], 1):
        if v != cur_v:
            run_lens_t[idx - cur_start] += 1
            cur_v, cur_start = v, idx
    run_lens_t[1024 - cur_start] += 1

print("\nTuned 4KB-step word XOR unique count:", len(all_page_xors_t))
print("Tuned top 12 4KB-step word XORs:", [(hex(k), v) for k, v in all_page_xors_t.most_common(12)])
print("Tuned run lengths (word_len: count):", sorted(run_lens_t.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1])[:15])
```

Here are the findings from running the container parser, payload profiler, and 4 KB page-delta checks against **`EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`** and **`EM43A.OsirisROM`**.

### Exact 4 KB Run Boundaries (`0x0C0000` vs. `0x0C1000` in Stock)

* Words `0..20` (`+0x0000..+0x0053`, 21 words): `0xdb8d1170`
* Words `21..225` (`+0x0054..+0x0387`, 205 words): `0xd781ea99`
* Words `226..430` (`+0x0388..+0x06BB`, 205 words): `0xd385bc3e`
* Words `431..635` (`+0x06BC..+0x09EF`, 205 words): `0xd781ea99`
* Words `636..839` (`+0x09F0..+0x0D1F`, 204 words): `0xdb8d1170`
* Words `840..1023` (`+0x0D20..+0x0FFF`, 184 words): `0xe7b2053d`

Notice that the transition offsets (`+0x0054`, `+0x0388`, `+0x06BC`, `+0x09F0`, `+0x0D20`) fall at non-power-of-two byte positions (`84`, `904`, `1724`, `2544`, `3360`) without any visible byte alignment marker in the ciphertext at those boundaries.

### `EM43A.OsirisROM` vs. `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM` Across 4 KB Steps

* **Distinct 4 KB-step word XORs (`0x0C0000..0x0FF000`):** `751` unique 32-bit XORs in `EM43A.OsirisROM` versus `22` in `EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`.
* **Shared dominant XOR constants:** The top constants (`0xe7b2053d`, `0xf7a35fa1`, `0xfbafa448`, `0xe3b6539a`, `0xdb8d1170`, `0xd781ea99`, `0xf3a70906`, `0xd385bc3e`) appear in both files with similar power-of-two/high-multiplicity counts (e.g., `0xdb8d1170` × `4,096` and `0xd781ea99` × `4,096` in `EM43A.OsirisROM`).
* **Run-length distribution in `EM43A.OsirisROM`:** Long runs of `205` (`185` times), `204` (`62` times), `144` (`61` times), and `61` (`61` times) words still recur across 4 KB page steps, interspersed with `776` single-word (`1`-word) breaks where tuned calibration modifications alter individual words.

### Takeaway

The non-power-of-two run lengths (`205`, `204`, `144`, `61` words) combined with identical word-XOR deltas across aligned offsets and power-of-two hit counts are characteristic of a structured stream/block cipher or PRNG keystream (such as a block/stream mode with fixed-stride keystream or constant plaintext regions) rather than a simple repeating short key or raw unmasked ROM.

Would you like to check whether `pay_s ^ pay_t` (the byte/word XOR between the stock and tuned `ROMData` payloads) shows zero-XOR runs where stock and tuned bytes match, or test modular/arithmetic word relationships across the `205`-word runs?


```python
import pathlib, struct
from collections import Counter

raw_s = pathlib.Path("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
raw_t = pathlib.Path("EM43A.OsirisROM").read_bytes()

w_t = struct.unpack(">262144I", pay_t)

zero_words = sum(1 for x in w_xor_st if x == 0)

print(f"Identical bytes in ROMData: {zero_bytes}/{len(xor_st)} ({zero_bytes/len(xor_st)*100:.4f}%)")

print("Top 15 repeated u32 word XORs between Stock and Tuned ROMData:", [(hex(k), v) for k, v in c_w_xor.most_common(15)])

# Check runs of identical w_xor_st values across the entire 1MB (262144 words)
runs_st = []
cur_v, cur_start = w_xor_st[0], 0
for idx, v in enumerate(w_xor_st[1:], 1):
    if v != cur_v:
        runs_st.append((cur_start, idx - 1, idx - cur_start, hex(cur_v)))
        cur_v, cur_start = v, idx
runs_st.append((cur_start, len(w_xor_st) - 1, len(w_xor_st) - cur_start, hex(cur_v)))

print("Total word-XOR runs across full 1MB (Stock vs Tuned):", len(runs_st))

# Run 1 is words 21..225 (205 words)
diffs_add = [((w1_s[i] - w0_s[i]) & 0xFFFFFFFF) for i in range(21, 226)]
xors_sub = [w1_s[i] ^ w0_s[i] for i in range(21, 226)]
print("\nStock run (words 21..225): unique XORs =", len(set(xors_sub)), "unique (w1-w0)%2^32 =", len(set(diffs_add)))
print("First 8 words of w0_s[21:29]:", [hex(x) for x in w0_s[21:29]])
print("First 8 words of w1_s[21:29]:", [hex(x) for x in w1_s[21:29]])
```

Running cross-ROM differential and arithmetic checks between **`EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM`** and **`EM43A.OsirisROM`** (using `osiris_rom_inspector_2.py` in our previous steps) confirms that the two files are masked with distinct per-file/per-tune keystreams rather than sharing a common keystream or unmasked flat ROM layout.

### Stock vs. Tuned `ROMData` Differential Findings

* **Zero Identical 32-Bit Words:** Out of `262,144` big-endian 32-bit words (`0x100000` bytes), **0 words** (`0.0000%`) are identical between Stock and Tuned `ROMData`, while `4,225 / 1,048,576` single bytes (`0.4029%`) match by chance (~`1/256` random byte collision rate).
* **No Long Identical XOR Runs Across Stock vs. Tuned:** Across all `262,144` word XORs (`w_s[i] ^ w_t[i]`), there are `254,181` runs with a maximum run length of only **2 words**, though specific 32-bit XOR values recur hundreds of times (`0xb6cd6172` × `617`, `0x619cfced` × `616`, `0x8c22dec7` × `610`, `0x8a220239` × `605`, `0x5d739fa6` × `598`).
* **Bitwise XOR vs. Modular Addition Across Strides:** Within Stock words `21..225` (`0x0C0000` vs. `+0x1000`), `w1_s[i] ^ w0_s[i]` is constant (`1` unique XOR value across all `205` words), whereas `(w1_s[i] - w0_s[i]) mod 2^32` produces `205` distinct values—confirming XOR-based masking over constant plaintext words across that page step.

### Practical Implications for `EM43A` Extraction

* Because `ROMData` in both `.OsirisROM` containers is XOR/stream-masked with per-file/per-tune parameters (and `Keys` differs between the Stock and Tuned files), neither `ROMData` slice should be fed directly into flat SH705x table/axis scanners without unmasking.


* The recurring 32-bit word XORs across aligned 4 KB / 64 KB / 256 KB strides and the power-of-two residue counts in `0x0C0000..0x100000` provide clean known-delta anchors if you want to model or solve the keystream/block schedule across erased/constant flash regions.

Would you like to examine how those recurring cross-file word XORs (`0xb6cd6172`, `0x619cfced`, etc.) distribute across 64 KB blocks, or inspect the exact byte/word layout of the `Keys` sections (`12` bytes each)?
```python
import pathlib, struct
from collections import Counter, defaultdict

raw_s = pathlib.Path("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
raw_t = pathlib.Path("EM43A.OsirisROM").read_bytes()
pay_s = raw_s[116 : 116 + 0x100000]
pay_t = raw_t[116 : 116 + 0x100000]

w_s = struct.unpack(">262144I", pay_s)
w_t = struct.unpack(">262144I", pay_t)
w_xor_st = [a ^ b for a, b in zip(w_s, w_t)]

top_xors = [k for k, _ in Counter(w_xor_st).most_common(10)]
print("Top 10 cross-file u32 XORs and their per-64KB block counts (16 blocks of 16384 words):")
for val in top_xors:
    per_blk = [0] * 16
    indices = []
    for idx, x in enumerate(w_xor_st):
        if x == val:
            per_blk[idx // 16384] += 1
            if len(indices) < 8:
                indices.append((idx, f"0x{idx*4:06X}", f"0x{w_s[idx]:08X}", f"0x{w_t[idx]:08X}"))
    print(f"  0x{val:08X}: total={sum(per_blk):3d} per_64KB={per_blk}")
    print(f"    samples (w_idx, off_hex, w_s, w_t): {indices[:4]}")

# Inspect Keys sections in detail (12 bytes each)
for label, raw in [("EM43A Back To Stock.OsirisROM", raw_s), ("EM43A.OsirisROM", raw_t)]:
    # Keys record is at 0x100078: tag 'Keys    ' (8B), plen=12 (4B), tlen=36 (4B), flags=1 (4B), payload (12B), trailer (4B)
    k_off = 0x100078
    tag = raw[k_off:k_off+8]
    plen, tlen, flags = struct.unpack_from("<III", raw, k_off + 8)
    k_bytes = raw[k_off + 20 : k_off + 20 + plen]
    k_trail = raw[k_off + 20 + plen : k_off + tlen]
    u32_be = struct.unpack(">3I", k_bytes)
    u32_le = struct.unpack("<3I", k_bytes)
    u16_be = struct.unpack(">6H", k_bytes)
    u16_le = struct.unpack("<6H", k_bytes)
    print(f"\n=== {label} Keys Section ===")
    print(f"  Raw payload (12B): {k_bytes.hex()} | trailer (4B): {k_trail.hex()}")
    print(f"  3x u32_be: {[hex(x) for x in u32_be]}")
    print(f"  3x u32_le: {[hex(x) for x in u32_le]}")
    print(f"  6x u16_be: {[hex(x) for x in u16_be]}")
    print(f"  6x u16_le: {[hex(x) for x in u16_le]}")


```
